## Chapter 12 answer key: Syllogisms

Every answer below was checked by listing every possible Venn picture of the groups by computer (each group has at least one member; "some" means at least one). Hand-written reasoning is given alongside.

### Self-check answers

1. **No.** "All laptops are devices" puts laptops inside devices. Devices could include tablets or phones, so the reverse is not forced (a picture with a non-laptop device obeys the statement).
2. **No.** The chipped mugs and the cheap chipped items may be different items, so no mug need be cheap. Two "Some" statements do not join up.
3. **(b).** "Only managers can approve refunds" means everyone who can approve refunds is a manager. It does not say every manager can approve (some managers may not).

### Practice answers

#### Q1 🟢
**Answer: I and III follow.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - All coupons are offers. means: circle coupons lies inside circle offers.
   - All offers are discounts. means: circle offers lies inside circle discounts.
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. All coupons are discounts. FOLLOWS: true in all 8 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - II. All discounts are coupons is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of coupons, offers and discounts only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of discounts only; a member of coupons, offers and discounts only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - III. Some discounts are coupons. FOLLOWS: true in all 8 possible pictures (checked by computer).
3. Idea in one line: Chain the two 'All' statements: coupons sit inside offers, offers sit inside discounts. Reversing an 'All' is never allowed, but because coupons exist and all of them are discounts, at least one discount is a coupon.

#### Q2 🟢
**Answer: I and III follow.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - Some nurses are night-shift workers. means: circles nurses and night-shift workers overlap (at least one member sits in both).
   - All night-shift workers are rota members. means: circle night-shift workers lies inside circle rota members.
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. Some nurses are rota members. FOLLOWS: true in all 32 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - II. All nurses are rota members is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of nurses, night-shift workers and rota members only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of nurses only; a member of nurses, night-shift workers and rota members only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - III. Some rota members are night-shift workers. FOLLOWS: true in all 32 possible pictures (checked by computer).
3. Idea in one line: The nurses who work nights are inside the night-shift circle, which is inside the rota circle. So those nurses are on the rota. Only the night-shift nurses are guaranteed, not all nurses.

#### Q3 🟢
**Answer: Only III follows.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - No samples are paid orders. means: circles samples and paid orders do not touch.
   - All paid orders are invoiced items. means: circle paid orders lies inside circle invoiced items.
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. No samples are invoiced items is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of samples only; a member of paid orders and invoiced items only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of samples and invoiced items only; a member of paid orders and invoiced items only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - II. Some invoiced items are samples is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of samples and invoiced items only; a member of paid orders and invoiced items only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of samples only; a member of paid orders and invoiced items only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - III. Some samples are not paid orders. FOLLOWS: true in all 12 possible pictures (checked by computer).
3. Idea in one line: Paid orders are inside invoiced items and are far away from samples. But invoiced items could be bigger than paid orders, and a sample could sit in the extra part. So I and II are only possible. Samples exist and none is a paid order, so III follows.

#### Q4 🟢
**Answer: None of the conclusions follows.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - Some vendors are online sellers. means: circles vendors and online sellers overlap (at least one member sits in both).
   - Some online sellers are wholesalers. means: circles online sellers and wholesalers overlap (at least one member sits in both).
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. Some vendors are wholesalers is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of vendors, online sellers and wholesalers only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of vendors and online sellers only; a member of online sellers and wholesalers only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - II. Some wholesalers are vendors is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of vendors, online sellers and wholesalers only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of vendors and online sellers only; a member of online sellers and wholesalers only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - III. Some vendors are not wholesalers is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of vendors and online sellers only; a member of online sellers and wholesalers only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of vendors, online sellers and wholesalers only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
3. Idea in one line: Two 'Some' statements never join up: the online sellers who are vendors may be different people from the online sellers who are wholesalers. So nothing definite links vendors and wholesalers.

#### Q5 🟢
**Answer: Only I follows.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - No pending tickets are closed tickets. means: circles pending tickets and closed tickets do not touch.
   - Some closed tickets are urgent tickets. means: circles closed tickets and urgent tickets overlap (at least one member sits in both).
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. Some urgent tickets are not pending tickets. FOLLOWS: true in all 24 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - II. No urgent tickets are pending tickets is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of pending tickets only; a member of closed tickets and urgent tickets only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of pending tickets and urgent tickets only; a member of closed tickets and urgent tickets only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - III. Some urgent tickets are pending tickets is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of pending tickets and urgent tickets only; a member of closed tickets and urgent tickets only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of pending tickets only; a member of closed tickets and urgent tickets only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
3. Idea in one line: The urgent tickets that are closed cannot be pending, so at least one urgent ticket is not pending. Other urgent tickets (not closed) might or might not be pending, so II and III are only possible.

#### Q6 🟢
**Answer: I follows, II possible, III false.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - All interns are trainees. means: circle interns lies inside circle trainees.
   - Some trainees are remote workers. means: circles trainees and remote workers overlap (at least one member sits in both).
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. Some remote workers are trainees. FOLLOWS: true in all 40 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - II. Some interns are remote workers is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of interns, trainees and remote workers only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of interns and trainees only; a member of trainees and remote workers only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - III. No interns are trainees. is DEFINITELY FALSE: false in all 40 possible pictures (checked by computer).
3. Idea in one line: I is the second statement turned round, so it is certain. II depends on whether the remote trainees happen to be interns. III says the opposite of the first statement.

#### Q7 🟢
**Answer: (b) All discount holders are students.**
1. Draw the original: circle discount holders lies inside circle students.
2. Compare each option with that picture:
   - (a) All students are discount holders. Gives a different picture.
   - (b) All discount holders are students. Same picture, so it matches.
   - (c) No discount holders are students. Gives a different picture.
   - (d) Some students are not discount holders. Gives a different picture.
3. Idea in one line: 'Only students are discount holders' means that if you hold the discount, you must be a student. So the discount-holder circle sits inside the students circle. Option (a) draws the circles the wrong way round.

#### Q8 🟢
**Answer: I and II follow.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - Some bakeries are not Sunday traders. means: at least one member of bakeries sits outside circle Sunday traders.
   - All bakeries are shops. means: circle bakeries lies inside circle shops.
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. Some shops are not Sunday traders. FOLLOWS: true in all 28 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - II. Some shops are bakeries. FOLLOWS: true in all 28 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - III. All shops are bakeries is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of Sunday traders only; a member of bakeries and shops only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of bakeries and shops only; a member of Sunday traders and shops only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
3. Idea in one line: The bakeries that are not Sunday traders are shops, so some shops are not Sunday traders. Bakeries exist and all are shops, so some shops are bakeries. All shops being bakeries is only one possible picture.

#### Q9 🟢
**Answer: II and III follow.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - No guests are staff. means: circles guests and staff do not touch.
   - No staff are vendors. means: circles staff and vendors do not touch.
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. No guests are vendors is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of guests only; a member of staff only; a member of vendors only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of staff only; a member of guests and vendors only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - II. Some guests are not staff. FOLLOWS: true in all 10 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - III. Some staff are not vendors. FOLLOWS: true in all 10 possible pictures (checked by computer).

#### Q10 🟢
**Answer: Only II follows.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - All taxis are cars. means: circle taxis lies inside circle cars.
   - Some cars are electric vehicles. means: circles cars and electric vehicles overlap (at least one member sits in both).
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:

In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of taxis and cars only; a member of cars and electric vehicles only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - II. Some electric vehicles are cars. FOLLOWS: true in all 40 possible pictures (checked by computer).

In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of taxis, cars and electric vehicles only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.

#### Q11 🟢
**Answer: (a) No expired offers are coupons.**
1. Draw the original: circles coupons and expired offers do not touch.
2. Compare each option with that picture:
   - (a) No expired offers are coupons. Same picture, so it matches.
   - (b) All expired offers are coupons. Gives a different picture.
   - (c) Some coupons are expired offers. Gives a different picture.
   - (d) Some expired offers are not coupons. Gives a different picture.
3. Idea in one line: 'No A are B' is symmetric: the two circles do not touch, whichever one you name first. Option (d) is also true here (expired offers exist and none is a coupon), but it is a weaker claim: it would still be true if some expired offers were coupons, so it does not say the same thing. Only (a) says the two groups never meet.

#### Q12 🟢
**Answer: 6.**
1. Organic items sit inside green-labelled items, so all 12 organic items are among the 18 green-labelled ones.
2. Green-labelled items that are not organic: 18 minus 12 = 6.
3. Check: 12 organic (all green) + 6 green non-organic = 18 green; 30 minus 18 = 12 items with no green label. Total 12 + 6 + 12 = 30. (I also checked every possible split of the 30 items by computer: 6 is the only answer.)

#### Q13 🟢
**Answer: Only II follows.**
1. Draw one circle per group and translate each statement:
   - Some plans are not approved items. means: at least one member of plans sits outside circle approved items.
   - All approved items are funded items. means: circle approved items lies inside circle funded items.
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:

   - All cooks are adults. means: circle cooks lies inside circle adults.
   - Some adults are clients. means: circles adults and clients overlap (at least one member sits in both).
   - No clients are owners. means: circles clients and owners do not touch.
2. Draw the loosest picture the statements allow, then test each conclusion against every picture:
   - I. Some owners are adults. FOLLOWS: true in all 192 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - II. Some clients are not cooks is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of owners, cooks and adults only; a member of adults and clients only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of owners, cooks and adults only; a member of cooks, adults and clients only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
   - III. Some owners are not clients. FOLLOWS: true in all 192 possible pictures (checked by computer).
   - IV. Some cooks are clients is only POSSIBLE. In one picture it is true (the picture holds: a member of owners, cooks and adults only; a member of cooks, adults and clients only). In another it is false (the picture holds: a member of owners, cooks and adults only; a member of adults and clients only). Both obey the statements, so it does not follow.
3. Idea in one line: Owner-cooks are adults (I). Owners are never clients, so any owner is a non-client (III). Clients who are not cooks (II), and cooks who are clients (IV), are only possible: the statements neither force nor forbid them.

#### Q40 🔴
**Answer: statement (4), "Some staff are not riders."**
1. Riders sit inside couriers, couriers inside staff, and staff never meet guests. So riders never meet guests: statements (1), (2), (3) alone give the conclusion.
2. Remove (1): the link from riders to couriers is lost, so riders could be guests. Needed.
3. Remove (2): couriers may leave staff, so a rider could be a guest. Needed.
4. Remove (3): staff may touch guests, so the chain breaks. Needed.
5. Remove (4): the conclusion still holds, because (4) says nothing that links riders to guests. It is not needed.
6. Checked by computer: removing any one of (1), (2), (3) lets the conclusion fail; removing (4) does not.

## Chapter 13: Direction and Distance

Notation: position is (across, up) from the start (0, 0); east and north are plus, west and south are minus. Compass order clockwise: N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W, NW (45 degrees each).

### Self-check answers

1. **East.** A right turn from north is a quarter turn clockwise, which gives east.
2. **0 km.** 5 km north takes you to (0, 5); 5 km south brings you back to (0, 0). You walked 10 km but are at the start.
3. Distance travelled is the sum of every leg walked; distance from the start is the straight line between start and end, which can be much smaller.

### Practice answers

**Q1** Position: 24 east then 10 north gives (24, 10). Straight line: 24 and 10 are 2 times 12 and 5, so 2 times 13 = 26 km. Across plus, up plus. **26 km, north-east of the depot.**

**Q2** East, turn right (cw) gives south; turn right again gives west. **West.**

**Q3** North-west is one step before north in clockwise order. A 90 degree turn is two steps cw: NW to N to NE. **North-east.**

**Q4** Put G at (0, 0). S is 15 m west: (-15, 0). O is 20 m south of S: (-15, -20). Across 15, up 20 is 5 times (3, 4), so 5 times 5 = 25 m. Both minus. **25 m, south-west of G.**

**Q5** The question does not say which way she faces at the start; it says she walks south, so she faces south. 18 south gives (0, -18). Left turn from south faces east: 5 east gives (5, -18). Left turn from east faces north: 6 north gives (5, -12). Straight line: 5 and 12 give 13 km (friendly triple 5, 12, 13). Across plus, up minus. Walked: 18 + 5 + 6 = 29 km. **13 km, south-east of the start; walked 29 km.**

**Q6** Morning: sun in the east, shadow falls west. Shadow is on her left, so her left is west. Facing north, left is west. **North.**

**Q7** Facing south, walk 10 m: (0, -10). Left turn from south faces east: 5 m gives (5, -10). The mailroom (0, 0) is 5 m west and 10 m north of him: **north-west of him**. Walked 10 + 5 = **15 m**.

**Q8** Anticlockwise order from south: S, SE, E. One step (45 degrees) acw from south is south-east. **South-east.**

**Q9** Y at (0, 0), X at (20, 0), Z at (0, 15). From X to Z: 20 west and 15 north. 20 and 15 are 5 times 4 and 3, so 5 times 5 = 25 km. Z is to the west and north of X. **25 km, Z is north-west of X.**

**Q10** Asha at (0, 11), Babu at (0, -5). Both on the same north-south line: 11 + 5 = 16 km apart. Asha is further north. **16 km, Asha is due north of Babu.**

**Q11** Facing north, 14 km: (0, 14). Right gives east, 16 km: (16, 14). Right from east gives south, 26 km: (16, -12). Straight line: 16 and 12 are 4 times 4 and 3, so 4 times 5 = 20 km. Across plus, up minus. **20 km, south-east of the start.**

**Q12** Facing west, 8 km: (-8, 0). Left from west gives south, 15 km: (-8, -15). Left from south gives east, 8 km: (0, -15). Across is zero, so she is 15 km due south of home. Walked: 8 + 15 + 8 = 31 km. **15 km due south of home; walked 31 km.**

**Q13** Use bearings in degrees clockwise from north: NE = 45. Plus 135 gives 180. Minus 270 (anticlockwise) gives -90, which equals 270 (west). Plus 45 gives 315. 315 is north-west. **North-west.**

**Q14** Evening: sun in the west, shadow falls east. Shadow on his left, so his left is east. Facing south, left is east. **South.**

**Q15** Gita: 18 km/h for 1.5 hours = 27 km north: (0, 27). Hari: 18 km/h for 2 hours = 36 km west: (-36, 0). Difference: 36 across, 27 up; 36 and 27 are 9 times 4 and 3, so 9 times 5 = 45 km. Hari is west and south of Gita. **45 km apart, Hari is south-west of Gita.**

**Q16** Facing NE (45 degrees). Left is a quarter turn anticlockwise: 45 minus 90 = -45, which is 315, north-west. Behind is a half turn: 45 + 180 = 225, south-west. **Omar is north-west of Neha; Pia is south-west of Neha.**

**Q17** Walked: 8 + 15 = 23 km. Position (-8, -15); straight line: 8 and 15 give 17 (friendly triple 8, 15, 17). Difference 23 - 17 = **6 km shorter**.

**Q18** Bearing: NW = 315. 90 acw: 315 - 90 = 225 (SW). 135 cw: 225 + 135 = 360, which is 0 (N). 225 acw: 0 - 225 = -225, which is 135 (SE). **South-east.**

**Q19** Facing east, 6 km: (6, 0). Left from east faces north, 8 km: (6, 8). Left from north faces west, 14 km: (-8, 8). Right from west faces north, 7 km: (-8, 15). She now faces north. Straight line: 8 and 15 give 17 km. Across minus, up plus. **17 km, north-west of the start, facing north.**

**Q20** Anil: 16 north, 12 east: (12, 16). Straight line: 12 and 16 are 4 times 3 and 4, so 20 m. Beena: 9 west, 12 south: (-9, -12). 9 and 12 are 3 times 3 and 4, so 15 m. Beena is nearer by 20 - 15 = 5 m. Between them: across 12 - (-9) = 21, up 16 - (-12) = 28; 21 and 28 are 7 times 3 and 4, so 35 m. Anil is east and north of Beena. **Beena is nearer by 5 m; they are 35 m apart; Anil is north-east of Beena.**

**Q21** Facing north, 10 km: (0, 10). Right (east), 7 km: (7, 10). Left from east is north, 6 km: (7, 16). Left from north is west, 22 km: (-15, 16). Right from west is north, 4 km: (-15, 20). Facing north. Straight line: 15 and 20 are 5 times 3 and 4, so 25 km. Across minus, up plus. **25 km, north-west of the start, facing north.**

**Q22** After 12 km east she is at (12, 0). Left from east is north, so she ends at (12, d). Distance to depot: 12 squared plus d squared = 20 squared, so d squared = 400 - 144 = 256, d = 16 (distance cannot be negative). Total travelled: 12 + 16 = 28 km. She is east and north of the depot. **16 km after the turn; 28 km in total; north-east of the depot.**

**Q23** Morning: shadows fall west. Tara's shadow is in front of her, so Tara faces west. Back to back means Uday faces the opposite way: east. His shadow also falls west, which is behind him. Tara turns 90 degrees anticlockwise from west: west, then south. **Uday faces east, his shadow falls behind him; Tara walks south.**

**Q24** B (0, 0). A: 4 north gives (0, 4). C: 6 east of A gives (6, 4). D: 8 south of C gives (6, -4). E: 6 west of D gives (0, -4). E is 4 m due south of B. E to C: across 6, up 8 (4 + 4); 6 and 8 give 10. **E is 4 m due south of B; E is 10 m from C.**

**Q25** The 12 points north. At 4:30 the minute hand is on the 6, which points south. The hour hand is halfway between 4 and 5: 4.5 hours times 30 degrees = 135 degrees clockwise from north, which is south-east. Angle between south (180) and south-east (135) is 45 degrees. **Minute hand south, hour hand south-east, 45 degrees apart.**

**Q26** Bearings: east = 90. 90 acw: 90 - 90 = 0 (north). Some clockwise angle x: 0 + x = x. Then 45 acw: x - 45. This must equal south-west = 225, so x = 270. Check: 270 is less than 360, and no other value below 360 works. **270 degrees clockwise** (the same as a quarter turn anticlockwise).

**Q27** Leg 1: 24 minutes at 30 km/h = 0.4 hour times 30 = 12 km south: (0, -12). Right from south faces west. Leg 2: 40 minutes at 24 km/h = two thirds of an hour times 24 = 16 km west: (-16, -12). Straight back: 16 and 12 give 20 km. Time back: 20 km at 40 km/h = 0.5 hour = 30 minutes. Total: 24 + 40 + 30 = 94 minutes. The hub is east and north of him. **94 minutes (1 hour 34 minutes); the hub is north-east of the rider.**

**Q28** Legs 2, 5, 8, 11, 14, turning right after each one except the last, starting north. Headings: north, east, south, west, north.
- 2 north: (0, 2)
- 5 east: (5, 2)
- 8 south: (5, -6)
- 11 west: (-6, -6)
- 14 north: (-6, 8)

Straight line: 6 and 8 give 10 km. Across minus, up plus. **10 km, north-west of the start.**

**Q29** X (0, 12), Y (9, 0), Z (-16, 0).
- X to Y: across 9, up 12; 3 times (3, 4) gives 15 km.
- X to Z: across 16, up 12; 4 times (4, 3) gives 20 km.
- Y to Z: both on the east-west line: 9 + 16 = 25 km.

The largest is 25 km. **Y and Z are farthest apart at 25 km; X to Y is 15 km; X to Z is 20 km.**

**Q30** Work backward from the end. Final bearing 0 (north). Undo the last turn (45 acw): add 45, giving 45. Undo 90 cw: subtract 90, giving -45, which is 315. Undo 135 acw: add 135, giving 450, which is 90 (east). Check forward: 90 - 135 = -45 (315); +90 = 405, which is 45; -45 = 0, north. **East.**

## Chapter 14: Blood Relations and Family Trees

Conventions used throughout (as stated in the chapter): siblings are full siblings, each person has one marriage, every marriage is between one man and one woman, nobody marries a blood relative, "husband" means a man and "wife" means a woman, and the family described is the whole family. Every chain answer below was checked by building the family in a small program and reading the relation off it. The multi-person puzzles (Q35, Q38, Q39) were checked by exhaustive search.

---

### Self-check answers

1. **Aunt** (your maternal uncle's wife). Path: mother, then her brother, then his wife. She is related to you by marriage, not by blood.
2. **No.** "A is the brother of B" tells you A is male and that A and B share parents. B could be a boy or a girl.
3. "Only" means **exactly one person**. "My father's brother" could be several men, so "his wife" would not be definite. "My father's only brother" is one man, so you can keep following the chain.

---

### Easy

**Q1.** Hana says, "Leo is the husband of my father's only sister."
1. Hana's father (M).
2. His only sister: Hana's paternal aunt (F).
3. Her husband is Leo.
4. The husband of a parent's sister is an uncle by marriage.

**Answer: Leo is Hana's uncle (her father's sister's husband).** Check: Hana is Leo's niece by marriage.

**Q2.** Neil's wife is Olga (F), Olga is the only daughter of Pavel, so Pavel is Olga's father. Quinn is Neil's son, so Quinn is also Olga's son (one marriage). Pavel is the father of Quinn's mother.

**Answer: Pavel is Quinn's maternal grandfather.**

**Q3.** Wen's wife is Xia. Xia's only sister is Yan, so Yan is the sister of Wen's wife.

**Answer: Yan is Wen's sister-in-law.**

**Q4.** Count by group: Rosa = 1; her two sons = 2; their two wives = 2; grandchildren: 2 couples × 2 children = 4. Total: 1 + 2 + 2 + 4 = **9 people.**

**Q5.** Joel is the son of Lena's brother. So Joel's father is Lena's brother, and Lena is the sister of Joel's father. A father's sister (Lena is a woman) is a paternal aunt.

**Answer: Lena is Joel's paternal aunt.**

**Q6.** Cole's mother has exactly two children, Cole and Dina, so Cole and Dina are siblings. Eli is Dina's husband (M), so Eli is the husband of Cole's sister.

**Answer: Eli is Cole's brother-in-law.** (This holds whether Cole is a boy or a girl.)

**Q7.** Zoya's mother's only daughter is a woman who is a daughter of Zoya's mother. Zoya is also a daughter of that mother (she is a woman), and "only daughter" means there is just one. So the "only daughter" is **Zoya herself**. The man is the husband of Zoya.

**Answer: He is Zoya's husband.**

**Q8.** Gus is the husband of Sam's father's mother (Sam's paternal grandmother). So Gus is the husband of Sam's paternal grandmother.

**Answer: Gus is Sam's paternal grandfather.**

**Q9.** A is a sister (A female). B is a son (B male) of C, and C is the wife of D, so D is the husband of B's mother, which is B's father. A is B's sister, so A shares the same father.

**Answer: D is A's father.**

**Q10.** Grandchildren: 2 + 2 + 1 = **5**. Descendants: 3 children + 5 grandchildren = **8**.

**Q11.** Aisha's husband is the son of Aisha's mother-in-law. If the mother-in-law has exactly one son, that son is Aisha's husband.

**Answer: He is Aisha's husband.**

**Q12.** Tess's mother (1), her mother (2), her mother (3): Tess's mother's mother's mother.

**Answer: Una is Tess's maternal great-grandmother.**

**Q13.** A man's brother-in-law can be: (1) his **wife's brother** (spouse's sibling); (2) his **sister's husband** (sibling's spouse). Two different people, both called brother-in-law. (For a woman, the same word also covers her **husband's brother**. In some families a wife's sister's husband is also casually called a brother-in-law, but this chapter does not use that.)

**Q14.** Olive is Nils's wife, and Nils is Mia's husband's father. So Olive is Mia's husband's mother.

**Answer: Olive is Mia's mother-in-law.**

---

### Medium

**Q15.** Each girl has 3 sisters, so there are 3 + 1 = **4 daughters**. Each girl has 2 brothers, so there are **2 sons**. Each boy has all 4 daughters as sisters, and 1 brother (the other son).

**Answer: 2 sons, 4 daughters; each boy has 4 sisters.** Check: a girl sees 3 sisters + 2 brothers = 5 siblings, total children = 6.

**Q16.** Mei's mother's only brother is Mei's maternal uncle (M). His only daughter is Mei's cousin (F). Jun is that cousin's husband.

**Answer: Jun is the husband of Mei's cousin.** (People sometimes say "cousin-in-law", but there is no standard single word, so describing the path is correct.)

**Q17.** Gus's wife (F). Her only brother (M) is Gus's brother-in-law. His son (M). That son's sister is a girl in the same family, so a daughter of the wife's brother.

**Answer: The girl is Gus's niece (his wife's brother's daughter).**

**Q18.** Gina's father's mother is Gina's paternal grandmother. Her sons' wives are her daughters-in-law; "only daughter-in-law" means there is one. Gina's own mother is married to the grandmother's son (Gina's father), so Gina's mother is the only daughter-in-law. The boy's mother is Gina's mother, so the boy is Gina's sibling and he is a boy.

**Answer: The boy is Gina's brother.** Check: Gina is the sister of the boy.

**Q19.** Sana's husband's mother is her mother-in-law. Her only brother is the husband's maternal uncle (M). His wife is the husband's aunt by marriage.

**Answer: The woman is Sana's husband's aunt (his mother's brother's wife), so Sana's aunt by marriage (aunt-in-law).** There is no single short English word for this, so describing the path is correct.

**Q20.** Ahmed is the father of Lina. Lina and Marco have a son, Nico, so Nico is Lina's son. Nico is the child of Ahmed's daughter.

**Answer: Nico is Ahmed's grandson (his daughter's son).**

**Q21.** Read each symbol as a link.
- A - B: A is the brother of B (A is male).
- B + C: B is the husband of C (B is male, C is female).
- C * D: C is the daughter of D (C is female; D's gender is not given).

(a) A is the brother of B, and B is the husband of C, so A is the brother of C's husband: **A is C's brother-in-law**.
(b) D is C's parent, and C is married to B, so D is B's wife's parent: **D is B's father-in-law or mother-in-law** (D's gender is not stated, so say "parent-in-law").

**Answer: (a) brother-in-law; (b) parent-in-law (father-in-law or mother-in-law, cannot tell which).**

**Q22.**
- M % N: M is the daughter of N (M is female).
- N $ O: N is the brother of O (N is male, so N is M's father).

O is a sibling of N, so O is a sibling of M's father. M is the daughter of N, and N is O's brother, so M is O's brother's daughter.

**Answer: M is O's niece.** (O's gender is not given, so O is M's uncle or aunt.)

**Q23.** Uma's husband's mother has a son (the husband). "Only son" means he is the husband himself. His only daughter is a daughter of Uma's husband and (one marriage) of Uma.

**Answer: The nominee is Uma's daughter.**

**Q24.** Omar is the brother of Paz, so Omar is male. Noor is Omar's child, so Omar is Noor's father. Paz is Omar's sibling, so Paz is Noor's father's sibling. The gender of Paz is not given.

**Answer: Paz is Noor's paternal uncle or aunt; the exact word cannot be named because Paz's gender is not stated.** Say "father's sibling".

**Q25.** Rosa's mother's only sister is Rosa's maternal aunt (F). Her husband is the aunt's husband, and his son is also the aunt's son (one marriage). The aunt is Rosa's mother's sister, so the son is the child of Rosa's mother's sibling.

**Answer: The man is Rosa's cousin.**

**Q26.** Pim and Quin are siblings (children of Ola). Rae is Pim's daughter and Sid is Quin's son, so Rae and Sid are children of siblings. Ola is the mother of the parents of both.

**Answer: Rae and Sid are cousins; Ola is the grandmother of both.** (Whether paternal or maternal depends on whether Pim and Quin are men or women, which is not given.)

**Q27.** Count by generation: 1 great-grandfather; 2 children; 4 grandchildren; 4 great-grandchildren (each of the 4 grandchildren has 1 child). Total people: 1 + 2 + 4 + 4 = **11**. Each grandchild's first cousins are the 2 grandchildren from the other child (children of the parent's sibling). Descendants: 11 − 1 = **10**.

**Answer: 11 people; 2 first cousins each; 10 descendants.**

**Q28.** Aidan is a man, so his mother's daughters are his sisters. His mother's son-in-law is the husband of one of those daughters, so the husband of Aidan's sister (only one). The girl's father is that man, so the girl is the daughter of Aidan's sister.

**Answer: The girl is Aidan's niece.** Check: Aidan is her maternal uncle.

**Q29.** Dee is Cal's wife. Eddy is Dee's brother, so Gale is Dee's brother's daughter, so she is Cal's wife's niece.

**Answer (first part): Gale is Cal's niece (by marriage).**
Eddy and Fran are siblings, Gale is Eddy's child and Hal is Fran's child, so they are children of siblings.

**Answer (second part): Gale and Hal are cousins.**

**Q30.**
- Kim's mother's only brother Lou: **blood relative** (maternal uncle).
- Lou's only son Max: **blood relative** (cousin).
- Max's wife Nia: related **by marriage only** (a cousin's wife).
- Nia's mother Ola: **no relation to Kim**. Her only link is to Nia, and Nia is Kim's cousin's wife, so there is no path from Kim to Ola through blood or through Kim's own marriage.

**Answer: blood: Lou, Max; marriage only: Nia; no relation: Ola.**

---

### Hard

**Q31.** Translate the chain P * Q - R / S * T one link at a time.
- P * Q: P is the mother of Q (P female).
- Q - R: Q is the sister of R (Q female; R is her sibling).
- R / S: R is the husband of S (R male, S female).
- S * T: S is the mother of T. T is the child of R and S (one marriage).

R is Q's sibling and male, so R is Q's brother and P's son (siblings share parents). R is T's father.

(a) P is the mother of R, and R is T's father, so P is **T's paternal grandmother**.
(b) Q is R's sister, and R is T's father, so Q is **T's paternal aunt**.

**Q32.** Translate X # Y @ Z $ W one link at a time.
- X # Y: X is the daughter of Y (X female).
- Y @ Z: Y is the brother of Z (Y male).
- Z $ W: Z is the father of W (Z male).

So Y and Z are brothers. X is Y's daughter and W is Z's child, so X and W are children of brothers.

**Answer: W is X's cousin.** Gender: **X is female** (daughter), **Y is male** (brother), **Z is male** (father), **W is not stated**, so W's gender cannot be stated.

**Q33.** Build the tree: Abel (M) is the father of Bina and Carl. Dena is Carl's wife, so Carl is male. Ezra (M) is the only son of Carl and Dena. Fia is Ezra's only sister, so Fia is also a child of Carl and Dena (F). Gil is Bina's husband, so Bina is female, Gil is male.

(a) Fia is Carl's daughter, so Abel's son's daughter: **Abel's granddaughter**.
(b) Gil is the husband of Bina, and Bina is Ezra's father's sister: **Gil is Ezra's uncle (his father's sister's husband)**.
(c) Dena is the wife of Bina's brother Carl: **Dena is Bina's sister-in-law**.
(d) Bina is the sister of Fia's father Carl: **Bina is Fia's paternal aunt**.

**Q34.**
(a) Quin is Rae's husband, so Quin is male. Pat is Quin's sibling, but Pat's gender is not given. So Pat is Rae's husband's sibling: **Rae's brother-in-law or sister-in-law (cannot tell which)**.
(b) Pat's wife is Tess, so Pat is a husband, therefore male. Now Pat is Rae's husband's brother: **brother-in-law**.
(c) Pat is Quin's brother (male sibling), Tess is Pat's wife: **Tess is Quin's sister-in-law (his brother's wife)**.

**Q35.** The smallest group has **4 people**.

Why 4 works. Let G be a woman with two daughters M and S, and let M have a daughter N.
```
        G
       / \
      M   S
      |
      N
```
- Grandmother: G (grandmother of N). That is 1.
- Mothers: G (mother of M and S) and M (mother of N). That is 2.
- Daughters: M, S and N. That is 3.
- Aunt: S (aunt of N). That is 1.
- Niece: N (niece of S). That is 1.
- Sisters: M and S are sisters of each other. That is 2.

Why fewer cannot work. An aunt and a niece need three people: the aunt, the niece, and the niece's parent who is the aunt's sister. The grandmother has to be two generations above the niece, so she cannot be any of those three (the aunt and the niece's mother are only one generation above the niece). So at least a fourth person is needed. A search over all small female-only family structures with 2 to 5 members found no solution with 2 or 3 people and solutions from 4 people.

**Q36.** Zed's father's mother is Zed's paternal grandmother. Her grandsons include Zed (a man, and a grandson of his father's mother). "The only grandson" means there is exactly one grandson, so it is **Zed himself**. The boy is the only son of Zed.

**Answer: The boy is Zed's son.**

**Q37.** Let s = sons and d = daughters, with s + d = 9.
- A daughter has d − 1 sisters and s brothers. "2 more brothers than sisters": s = (d − 1) + 2 = d + 1.
- A son has s − 1 brothers and d sisters. "As many brothers as sisters": s − 1 = d, so s = d + 1.

Both clues say the same thing: s = d + 1. With s + d = 9: (d + 1) + d = 9, so 2d = 8, d = 4, s = 5.

Check: a daughter has 3 sisters and 5 brothers (2 more brothers); a son has 4 brothers and 4 sisters (equal). Total 5 + 4 = 9. A search over all splits of 9 children confirmed this is the only one.

**Answer: 5 sons and 4 daughters.**

**Q38.**
1. Three generations with two people each. Pat's grandmother is Robin, so Robin is in the oldest generation and Pat in the youngest. Kim is Pat's sister, so Kim is also youngest, and female.
2. Jo is Kim's mother and Sam is Jo's husband. Parents and spouses are in the same generation, so Jo and Sam are the middle generation. That leaves Alex and Robin as the oldest generation.
3. Alex is Jo's father-in-law, so Alex is the father of Jo's husband Sam. Alex is male.
4. Jo cannot be a child of Alex and Robin (she would then be Sam's sister, and no one is married to a sibling). So Jo is married in from outside, and Sam's mother is the other person in the oldest generation: Robin (female, by "grandmother").
5. The youngest generation (Kim, Pat) are Sam and Jo's two children. Sam has exactly one son and one daughter. Kim is a daughter, so Pat is the son.

(a) **Robin** is Sam's mother.
(b) Pat is the son of Sam, and Sam is the son of Alex: **Pat is Alex's grandson (his son's son)**.
(c) Robin is Sam's mother and Sam is Jo's husband: **Robin is Jo's mother-in-law**.

A search over every possible family on these six people (genders and parent links) with these clues found exactly one family, matching the above.

**Q39.**
1. Three generations with two people each. Chris is the husband of Dana, so they are in the same generation. Dana is the grandmother of Gray and Harper, so Dana and Chris are the oldest, and Gray and Harper the youngest.
2. That leaves Eden and Frankie as the middle generation.
3. Frankie is the son-in-law of Chris: Frankie is male and married to a daughter of Chris. A son-in-law is not a blood relative, so Frankie is married in, and his wife is Chris's daughter in the middle generation: **Eden** (female).
4. Gray and Harper are siblings in the youngest generation, and their parents must be a couple from the middle generation: Frankie and Eden.

(a) **Gray's parents are Frankie and Eden.**
(b) **Eden is Chris's daughter.**
(c) **No.** Nothing says whether Gray is a boy or a girl. The search over all genders found four possibilities for Gray and Harper (boy/girl combinations), all consistent with the facts. So Gray could be Chris's grandson or granddaughter.

**Q40.** From statements 1 and 3: Ned is Olly's brother, so Ned and Olly share parents. Pia is Ned's sister, so Pia shares those same parents. That makes **Pia Olly's sister**. But statement 2 says Olly is Pia's father. A person cannot be both a sister and a daughter of the same man (and a father cannot share his own parents with his child). So **statements 1 and 3 together clash with statement 2**. All three cannot be true.

Repair: change one word in statement 3 so that Pia is Ned's **niece** (the daughter of his brother). Then: Ned is Olly's brother, Olly is Pia's father, Pia is Ned's niece. All three can be true: Olly is Pia's father and Ned is Pia's uncle.

## Chapter 15: Ranking, Ordering and Comparison

### Self-check answers

1. Rank from bottom = 20 minus 5 plus 1 = **16**. (4 people above Rita, 15 below, so she is 16th counting from the bottom.)
2. **Chitra** is the shortest (chain: Anu > Bijal > Chitra). Yes, Anu is taller than Chitra, because comparisons chain: taller than a person who is taller than Chitra means taller than Chitra.
3. Sorted: 90, 80, 80, 70. The two 80s share rank 2 and use places 2 and 3, so 70 sits in place 4. Rank **4**.

---

### Practice answers

#### Easy

**Q1.** Total 35, Kiran 8th from the top.
1. People above Kiran: 8 minus 1 = 7.
2. People below Kiran: 35 minus 7 minus 1 = 27.
3. Rank from bottom = 27 + 1 = 28.
Shortcut check: 35 minus 8 plus 1 = 28.
**Answer: 28th from the bottom.**

**Q2.** Add the two ranks: 12 + 19 = 31. Lata is counted in both, so subtract 1: 30.
Check: 11 above, 18 below, 11 + 1 + 18 = 30.
**Answer: 30 people.**

**Q3.** Mohan is 17th, so 16 people are above him. Below him: 50 minus 16 minus 1 = 33.
**Answer: 33 candidates scored lower.**

**Q4.** Ranks 6 and 15 from the same end. Difference 15 minus 6 = 9. Strictly between: 9 minus 1 = 8 (ranks 7 to 14).
**Answer: 8 people.**

**Q5.** Chain: Pooja > Qasim > Rhea. Rhea is the shortest. Chains carry through, so Pooja > Rhea is true.
**Answer: Rhea is shortest; yes, Pooja is taller than Rhea.**

**Q6.** Write heavier on the left: Sameer > Tara, Uday > Sameer, Vera > Uday, Tara > Wasim.
Join: Vera > Uday (Vera in front of Uday), Uday > Sameer, Sameer > Tara, Tara > Wasim. Chain: Vera > Uday > Sameer > Tara > Wasim. Every name appears once, so the order is fixed (checked by testing all 120 orders: only one fits).
**Answer: Vera, Uday, Sameer, Tara, Wasim (heaviest to lightest). Sameer's parcel is in the middle.**

**Q7.** Sort: Mira 91, Jai 85, Kavi 85, Ira 72, Lina 60.
Mira is 1st. Jai and Kavi share rank 2 and use places 2 and 3. The next place is 4, so Ira is 4th. Lina is 5th.
**Answer: Ira's rank is 4.**

**Q8.** Add: 9 + 14 = 23. Nikhil is counted twice, subtract 1: 22.
**Answer: 22 people.**

**Q9.** Sorted: 90, 85, 85, 80, 70.
Competition: 90 is place 1, the two 85s share rank 2 (places 2 and 3), so 80 is in place 4: rank 4.
Dense: distinct scores are 90, 85, 80, 70, so 80 is distinct number 3: rank 3.
**Answer: 4 under competition ranking, 3 under dense ranking.**

**Q10.** "7 places below" means the rank number is 7 bigger: Sana is 10 + 7 = 17th from the top. From the bottom: 40 minus 17 plus 1 = 24.
**Answer: 24th from the bottom.**

#### Medium

**Q11.** Varun scored higher, so he is above Anil.
1. Varun is 11th from the top. Nine candidates sit between, so Anil is 11 + 9 + 1 = 21st from the top.
2. Anil is 8th from the bottom, so 7 people are below him.
3. Total = 21 + 7 = 28.
Check: 28 minus 21 plus 1 = 8, matches.
**Answer: 28 candidates.**

**Q12.** After the swap, Bina takes Chetan's old place and Chetan takes Bina's.
(a) Bina is now 26th from the top. From the bottom: 40 minus 26 plus 1 = 15.
(b) The same two places are occupied (13th and 26th), so between them are 26 minus 13 minus 1 = 12 students.
**Answer: (a) 15th from the bottom; (b) 12 students.**

**Q13.** Write higher salary on the left: P > Q, Q > R, S > P, R > T, U > S.
Join: U > S > P > Q > R > T (U > S, S > P, P > Q, Q > R, R > T). All six names are in one chain, so the order is fixed (all 720 orders tested; only one fits).
Third highest: U, S, then P. Lowest: T.
**Answer: P earns the third highest; T earns the lowest.**

**Q14.** 11 people ahead means Meera is 12th from the top. 7 behind means she is 8th from the bottom. Total = 11 + 1 + 7 = 19. Check: 12 + 8 minus 1 = 19.
**Answer: 12th from the top, 8th from the bottom, 19 people.**

**Q15.** Final score = 2 times interview + written.
- Uday: 2 times 7 + 6 = 20.
- Vera: 2 times 5 + 12 = 22.
- Wasim: 2 times 9 + 3 = 21.
- Xena: 2 times 6 + 9 = 21.
Order by score: Vera 22, then Wasim and Xena tied at 21, then Uday 20. Tie-break: higher interview score goes first, Wasim has 9 and Xena has 6, so Wasim is ahead.
Order: Vera, Wasim, Xena, Uday.
**Answer: Wasim ranks 2nd; Uday ranks last.**

**Q16.** Work from the known person.
1. Jai is 22nd from the bottom: from the top 45 minus 22 plus 1 = 24.
2. Ira is 7 places above Jai: 24 minus 7 = 17.
3. Kavi is 9 places below Ira: 17 + 9 = 26.
Check: Ira 17, Jai 24 (7 apart), Kavi 26 (9 below Ira, 2 below Jai). All within 1 to 45.
**Answer: Kavi is 26th from the top.**

**Q17.** Sorted: 92, 92, 88, 88, 88, 75, 75, 60.
Competition: the two 92s take places 1 and 2 (rank 1). The three 88s take places 3, 4, 5 (rank 3). The two 75s take places 6 and 7 (rank 6). 60 takes place 8 (rank 8).
Dense: distinct scores in order are 92, 88, 75, 60, so ranks are 1, 2, 3, 4.
Sameer (75): competition 6, dense 3. Rhea (60): competition 8, dense 4.
**Answer: Sameer 6 (competition) and 3 (dense); Rhea 8 (competition) and 4 (dense).**

**Q18.** Improvement = Test 1 rank minus Test 2 rank (positive means moved up).
- Tara: 3 minus 1 = +2.
- Uday: 1 minus 3 = minus 2 (worse).
- Vera: 5 minus 2 = +3.
- Wasim: 2 minus 5 = minus 3 (worse).
- Xena: 4 minus 4 = 0 (same).
The biggest gain is Vera, +3. Worse ranks: Uday and Wasim, 2 trainees.
**Answer: Vera improved the most; 2 trainees ended with a worse rank.**

**Q19.** With 9 people, the middle one has 4 above and 4 below, so the median person is 5th from the top.
Gauri is 2 places above: 5 minus 2 = 3rd from the top. From the bottom: 9 minus 3 plus 1 = 7.
**Answer: 3rd from the top and 7th from the bottom.**

**Q20.** Finish order, earliest first. Clues: Tanvi < Ravi, Ravi < Sana, Sana < Varun, Anil between Tanvi and Ravi (Tanvi, Anil, Ravi).
Join: Tanvi, Anil, Ravi, Sana, Varun. All five names are in one chain (all 120 orders tested; only one fits).
4th place: Sana.
**Answer: Tanvi, Anil, Ravi, Sana, Varun; Sana finished 4th.**

#### Hard

**Q21.** Positions 1 (top) to 6.
1. Who can be 1st? Kiran is above Mohan and Pooja. Lata is below Mohan, Neha is below Lata. Omar is not 1st. So only Kiran can be 1st. Kiran is 1st.
2. Mohan and Lata form a block (Lata immediately below Mohan), with Neha somewhere below Lata.
3. Omar and Pooja are in places with exactly one between them, with Omar above: (2, 4), (3, 5) or (4, 6).
4. Try (2, 4): the free places are 3, 5, 6. The Mohan and Lata block needs two places in a row: 5 and 6. Then Neha has no place below Lata. Fail.
5. Try (3, 5): free places are 2, 4, 6, no two in a row. Fail.
6. Try (4, 6): free places are 2, 3, 5. Block at 2 and 3, Neha at 5 (below Lata). All clues hold.
Order: Kiran, Mohan, Lata, Omar, Neha, Pooja (checked by testing all 720 orders: only this one fits).
**Answer: Omar is ranked 4th.**

**Q22.** The five late candidates are all above Dhruv.
1. New total = 40 + 5 = 45.
2. Dhruv's rank from the top moves down by 5: 14 + 5 = 19.
3. Rank from the bottom = 45 minus 19 plus 1 = 27.
Check: the number of people below Dhruv has not changed (40 minus 14 = 26), so his bottom rank is 26 + 1 = 27.
**Answer: total 45; Dhruv is 27th from the bottom.**

**Q23.** Under competition ranking, a rank equals (people strictly ahead) + 1.
(a) Rank 4 means 3 people are strictly ahead of the rank 4 group.
(b) Rank 9 means 8 people are ahead of the rank 9 group. Those 8 are the 3 ahead of the rank 4 group, the 3 in the rank 4 group, and the rest. So between the two groups: 8 minus 3 minus 3 = 2 people.
(c) Total = 3 + 3 + 2 + 2 = 10 (the last group of 2 is at the bottom).
**Answer: (a) 3; (b) 2; (c) 10 people.**

**Q24.** Dense rank 3 means two distinct score levels are above Uma's level. Those levels hold 2 + 3 = 5 people. Under competition ranking, rank = people strictly ahead + 1 = 5 + 1 = 6. (How many share Uma's level does not matter, because tied people share the same rank.)
**Answer: 6.**

**Q25.** Sort by rating (high first), then attendance, then joining year.
- Rating 5: Vikram and Yash both have attendance 91. Tie. Yash joined 2018, Vikram 2021, so Yash first, Vikram second.
- Rating 4: Zara (98), then Uma and Wendy both 96. Tie on attendance. Wendy joined 2017, Uma 2019, so Wendy before Uma.
- Rating 3: Xavi and Ravi both 99. Xavi joined 2016, Ravi 2020, so Xavi first.
Order: Yash 1, Vikram 2, Zara 3, Wendy 4, Uma 5, Xavi 6, Ravi 7.
**Answer: Uma is 5th; Zara is 3rd; the third rule (earlier joining year) decided Wendy before Uma.**

**Q26.** Places 1 (first) to 7.
1. Hana is 2nd and Mina finished before Hana, so Mina is 1st.
2. Places 1 and 2 are taken, so Kali is in place 3 or later. Ivan is after Kali, and Lev is immediately after Kali, so Kali needs two later places: Kali is in places 3 to 5.
3. Lev is immediately after Kali. Exactly one runner is between Kali and Ivan, and Ivan is after Kali, so Ivan is two places after Kali.
4. Kali 3: Lev 4, Ivan 5. Places 6 and 7 for Nuri and Jori; Jori after Nuri, so Nuri 6, Jori 7. Nuri after Lev holds. All clues fit.
5. Kali 4: Lev 5, Ivan 6, leaving places 3 and 7 for Nuri and Jori. Nuri must be after Lev (place 5) so Nuri 7, but then Jori cannot be after Nuri. Fail.
6. Kali 5: Lev 6, Ivan 7, Nuri must be after Lev but place 7 is taken. Fail.
Order: Mina, Hana, Kali, Lev, Ivan, Nuri, Jori (checked by testing all 5,040 orders: only this one fits).
**Answer: Nuri finished 6th.**

**Q27.** Rank numbers per judge:

| Candidate | Judge 1 | Judge 2 | Judge 3 | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Alka | 1 | 2 | 3 | 6 |
| Bipin | 2 | 1 | 2 | 5 |
| Charu | 3 | 4 | 1 | 8 |
| Dhiraj | 4 | 5 | 4 | 13 |
| Eesha | 5 | 3 | 5 | 13 |

Lowest total first: Bipin 5, Alka 6, Charu 8. Dhiraj and Eesha tie at 13. Judge 1 ranked Dhiraj (4) above Eesha (5), so Dhiraj goes first.
**Answer: Bipin (5), Alka (6), Charu (8), Dhiraj (13), Eesha (13).**

**Q28.** Possible points per game: 3, 1 or 0. Each team plays 3 matches.
1. Bears have 1 point: the only way is one draw and two losses (no wins).
2. Lions have 7 points in 3 matches: 3 + 3 + 1, so two wins and one draw.
3. Wolves have 5 points: 3 + 1 + 1, so one win and two draws.
4. Tigers have 3 points: either one win and two losses, or three draws. Count the draws: Lions 1, Bears 1, Wolves 2 give 4 draw-appearances; each drawn match gives 2 appearances, so the total must be even. If Tigers had three draws the total would be 7, which is odd. So Tigers have one win and two losses, no draws.
5. Wolves have two draws, but Tigers have none, so Wolves drew with Lions and Bears. That uses up Lions' one draw and Bears' one draw.
6. Lions' other two games are wins, against Tigers and Bears. Bears lost their other two games (to Lions and to Tigers). Wolves' win must be against the Tigers (their only match left). Tigers' single win is over Bears.
Results: Lions beat Tigers, Lions beat Bears, Lions drew Wolves, Tigers beat Bears, Wolves beat Tigers, Bears drew Wolves (checked by testing all 729 result combinations: only this one gives these points).
**Answer: The draws were Lions v Wolves and Bears v Wolves; the Tigers were beaten by the Lions and by the Wolves.**

**Q29.** Track only the people below Suresh.
1. Originally 60 minus 24 = 36 candidates are below Suresh.
2. Disqualified candidates were above him, so they do not change the number below him.
3. Five late candidates are added below him: 36 + 5 = 41 below.
4. Rank from the bottom = 41 + 1 = 42.
Check with totals: new total 60 minus 8 plus 5 = 57; new rank from top 24 minus 8 = 16; 57 minus 16 plus 1 = 42.
**Answer: 42nd from the bottom.**

**Q30.** Notice that Yusuf's statement ("I finished first") and Xena's statement ("Yusuf finished first") say the same thing. So they are both true or both false.
1. If both were true, two statements would be true. But exactly one is true. So both are false.
2. Both false means Yusuf did not finish first.
3. Then the only true statement is Zubin's: Yusuf finished ahead of Zubin.
4. Yusuf is not 1st, and Yusuf is ahead of Zubin, so Yusuf is 2nd and Zubin 3rd. That leaves Xena 1st.
Order: Xena, Yusuf, Zubin. Check: Zubin's statement true, Yusuf's false (he is 2nd), Xena's false. Exactly one true (all six orders tested; only this one works).
**Answer: Xena 1st, Yusuf 2nd, Zubin 3rd.**

## Chapter 16 answer key: Seating Arrangements (Linear, Circular, Two-Row)

Every seating puzzle in this chapter (examples 1 to 4, scenario 1 and questions Q1 to Q6, Q8 to Q16, Q19, Q21, Q23 to Q28, Q30) was checked by trying every possible arrangement of the people in the seats and counting how many satisfy all the clues; each has exactly one valid arrangement (scenario 1 deliberately has several until its extra rules are added). Round-table puzzles were checked with one person's seat fixed, which is the same as ignoring rotations. Q20 and Q29 were counted by listing every arrangement by script. The queue and arithmetic questions (example 5, Q7, Q17, Q18, Q22, Q28's moves) were recomputed by script. Conventions are the ones in the chapter: rows face north (right = east), round tables face the centre (right = anticlockwise), top row faces south and bottom row faces north in two-row layouts.

### Self-check answers

1. Total = 5 + 3 - 1 = **7** people. (Ola is counted in both positions, so one is taken off.)
2. Opposite is half the table away: 3 seats round. The people strictly between you and them are **2** (going either way).
3. The bottom row west to east is Xan, Yeo, Zed. Ras is in seat 3 of the top row, and seat 3 of the bottom row is Zed. **Zed**.

### Easy

**Q1** Seat 1 is Kofi and seat 4 is Nia. Lena may not touch Kofi, so Lena cannot take seat 2; she takes seat 3. Mateo takes seat 2. Order: Kofi, Mateo, Lena, Nia. **Mateo**.

**Q2** Rosa is seat 3 and Omar, immediately left, is seat 2. Sven is at an end and must be left of Omar, so Sven is seat 1 (seat 5 is right of Omar). Petra and Quinn share seats 4 and 5; Petra may not touch Rosa, so Petra takes seat 5 and Quinn seat 4. Order: Sven, Omar, Rosa, Quinn, Petra. The east end is **Petra**.

**Q3** Place Tariq anywhere. Viktor takes the seat to Tariq's right (anticlockwise). Uma is directly opposite Tariq. Wen takes the last seat, which is Tariq's left-hand neighbour. Going anticlockwise: Tariq, Viktor, Uma, Wen. **Wen**.

**Q4** Zoe is 1st, Ingrid is 5th, Hugo is directly behind Zoe so 2nd. Yara and Xavi share places 3 and 4, and Yara is ahead of Xavi, so Yara is 3rd and Xavi 4th. Order: Zoe, Hugo, Yara, Xavi, Ingrid. People ahead of Xavi: **3**.

**Q5** Hana faces Greta, so Hana is bottom seat 3. Joel is not opposite Dalia (seat 1) and seat 3 is taken, so Joel is seat 2 and Ivo is seat 1. Felix is top seat 2, so the person opposite is Joel. Bottom row: Ivo, Joel, Hana. **Joel**.

**Q6** Leo is seat 1, Pia is seat 6. Mira is immediately right of Leo, seat 2. Otis is immediately left of Pia, seat 5. Kira and Noor share seats 3 and 4, and Kira is left of Noor, so Kira is seat 3 and Noor seat 4. Order: Leo, Mira, Kira, Noor, Otis, Pia. Seat 4 is **Noor**.

**Q7** (a) Total = 4 + 5 - 1 = **8** people. (b) From the back: 9 - 3 + 1 = **7th**.

**Q8** Place Jade anywhere. Imran is immediately right of Jade, so Imran is the next seat anticlockwise. Kai is immediately left of Jade, so Kai is the seat just before Jade (clockwise). That leaves two seats between Imran and Kai; Lars may not touch Imran, so Lars takes the one next to Kai and Mina takes the one next to Imran. Going anticlockwise from Jade: Jade, Imran, Mina, Lars, Kai. The seat immediately right of Imran is **Mina**.

**Q9** The north-side person faces south, so Wen's right-hand side is west and his left-hand side is east. Xavi is opposite Wen, on the south. Zoe is on Wen's right, the west side. The last seat, east, is Wen's left, and the remaining person is Yara. **Yara**.

**Q10** Test each plan against the four rules.
- A: Hana (seat 4) and Ivo (seat 5) are neighbours, breaking rule 3; Joel is in seat 6, breaking rule 4. Fails.
- B: Dalia is in seat 4, not an end, breaking rule 1. Fails.
- C: Dalia is at an end; Felix (4) and Greta (5) touch; Hana (6) and Ivo (2) are apart; Joel is in seat 3. Passes.
- D: Felix (2) and Greta (5) do not touch, breaking rule 2. Fails.
**Plan C**.

### Medium

**Q11** Jonas is seat 6. Nadia, Marek and Ines sit three in a row with Marek in the middle. Kaito is 3 seats right of Nadia. Try the options: if the trio fills seats 1 to 3 with Nadia in seat 1, Kaito is seat 4 and Lucia is seat 5, but Lucia must be left of Marek, so that fails. If Nadia is seat 3 then Kaito is seat 6, which Jonas has. If the trio fills seats 2 to 4 with Nadia in seat 2, Kaito is seat 5, Marek 3, Ines 4, and Lucia takes seat 1 (left of Marek). That works; no other start fits. Order: Lucia, Nadia, Marek, Ines, Kaito, Jonas. Immediately left of Kaito is **Ines**.

**Q12** Silas is seat 1. Exactly two between Silas and Oskar means Oskar is seat 4. Tessa is immediately right of Oskar, seat 5. Quentin is seat 7. Rhea is next to Silas, so seat 2. Paola is right of Tessa, so seat 6 (seat 7 is taken). Ugo takes seat 3. Order: Silas, Rhea, Ugo, Oskar, Tessa, Paola, Quentin. Between Ugo (3) and Paola (6): 6 - 3 - 1 = **2** people (Oskar and Tessa). (Clue 5 fixes Silas at the west end. Reading it the other way, with Silas at the east end, is not possible: clue 3 already puts Quentin there, and a script finds no arrangement at all.)

**Q13** Start at Gus and number the seats anticlockwise (right-hand direction): Gus 0, then 1, 2, 3, 4, 5. Dina is second right of Gus: seat 2. Emil is immediately left of Gus: seat 5. Bea is opposite Gus: seat 3. Cyrus and Freya take seats 1 and 4. Cyrus may not neighbour Gus, and seat 1 touches Gus, so Cyrus is seat 4 and Freya seat 1. Immediately right of Freya (seat 1) is seat 2, **Dina**.

**Q14** Start at Lior: seat 0, numbering anticlockwise (right-hand direction). Mei is third right: seat 3. Nico is immediately right of Mei: seat 4. Lior is second right of Orla, so Orla is 2 seats clockwise from Lior: seat 5. Pavel neighbours Orla; seat 4 is taken so Pavel is seat 6. Lior's neighbours are seats 1 and 6, and Quinn is not one of them, so Quinn is seat 2 and Ruth seat 1. Second right of Ruth (seat 1) is seat 3, **Mei**.

**Q15** Timo is top seat 1, Zelda is bottom seat 1, Yusuf is top seat 4. Wanda is in the top row; Ulla, opposite her, is in the bottom row. Sana is immediately east of Wanda in the same row, so Sana is also top, and the only two neighbouring free top seats are 2 and 3: Wanda 2, Sana 3. Ulla is bottom 2. Xiu is opposite Sana, bottom 3. Vikram has the last seat, bottom 4. Top: Timo, Wanda, Sana, Yusuf. Bottom: Zelda, Ulla, Xiu, Vikram. Opposite Yusuf is **Vikram**.

**Q16** Going round: West, North 1, North 2, East, South 2, South 1. Ilse is West, so Karim is East. Jorge is North 1, so Leona (opposite) is South 1. Milo and Nell take North 2 and South 2; Milo is on the north side, so Milo is North 2 and Nell South 2. Check clue 5: South 2 touches the East head, where Karim sits. Opposite Nell (South 2) is North 2, **Milo**.

**Q17** Start: Owen, Pru, Rafe, Sia, Tom. Pru and Tom swap: Owen, Tom, Rafe, Sia, Pru. Owen is served and leaves: Tom, Rafe, Sia, Pru. Uri joins: Tom, Rafe, Sia, Pru, Uri. (a) Third is **Sia**. (b) Rafe is 2nd and Uri is 5th: 5 - 2 - 1 = **2** people (Sia and Pru).

**Q18** (a) Mona has 6 people ahead. Two leave, so 4 remain ahead of her, and those 4 are served first at 3 minutes each: 4 x 3 = **12 minutes** (her service starts at minute 12). (b) With 4 people ahead she is **5th** from the front.

**Q19** Seat 1 is Una, seat 3 is Sofia, seat 5 is Tomek (clue 6). Remy and Vince share seats 2 and 4. The IT person is in seat 4 (immediately right of Sofia). Remy is HR, so Remy is not IT; therefore Vince is seat 4 and IT, and Remy is seat 2. Tomek, seat 5, is the Legal person. Una is not Finance and the only departments left for Una and Sofia are Finance and Sales, so Una is Sales and Sofia is Finance. Vince works in **IT**.

**Q20** Ivo has 2 choices (seat 1 or seat 4). The other three people fill the other three seats in 3 x 2 x 1 = 6 orders. Total 2 x 6 = **12** arrangements.

**Q21** Odile is in the top row, which faces south, so her right is west. Rania is immediately to her west, in the top row. Uri is opposite Rania, so Uri is in the bottom row. The bottom row faces north, so its right is east; Soren is immediately east of Uri, in the bottom row. Tilda is bottom seat 1, so Uri and Soren take seats 2 and 3 (Uri 2, Soren 3). Rania is opposite Uri, top seat 2, and Odile is immediately east of her, top seat 3. Pablo takes top seat 1. Opposite Odile (seat 3) is **Soren**.

**Q22** (a) Quinn is 3rd from the east end, so from the west he is 12 - 3 + 1 = 10th. Pat is 5th. People between: 10 - 5 - 1 = **4**. (b) Quinn moves two seats west to seat 8. Between seat 5 and seat 8: 8 - 5 - 1 = **2**.

### Hard

**Q23** Start at Fern (seat 0), numbering anticlockwise (the right-hand direction). Enzo is opposite: seat 4. Bryn is second right of Fern: seat 2. Hattie is immediately right of Bryn: seat 3. Delphi is immediately right of Enzo: seat 5. Free seats: 1, 6, 7, for Cosmo, Gideon and Ivor. Gideon may not neighbour Fern, and seats 1 and 7 are Fern's neighbours, so Gideon is seat 6. Cosmo must neighbour Gideon; seat 5 is taken, so Cosmo is seat 7 and Ivor seat 1. Immediately left of Fern is seat 7, **Cosmo**.

**Q24** Number the seats anticlockwise from Pia (seat 0). Pia faces outward, so her right is clockwise: Vera is seat 5. Rex is opposite Vera: seat 2. Rex faces outward, so his left is anticlockwise; second left of Rex means Sol is seat 4. Tove and Ugo take seats 1 and 3. Ugo is second right of Tove (Tove faces the centre, so right is anticlockwise): Tove 1, Ugo 3. Order: Pia, Tove, Rex, Ugo, Sol, Vera. Sol faces outward, so her right is clockwise: seat 3. **Ugo**.

**Q25** Mads is top 1; Nuru, opposite, is bottom 1; Pavla is immediately east of Nuru (the bottom row's right is east): bottom 2. Test where Raoul sits. If Raoul were in the bottom row, Orsi (opposite him) would be on top and Kenji would be east of Raoul in the bottom row; together with Nuru, Pavla, Quillan (Lola is on top, so Quillan is bottom) that puts five people in a row of four seats, impossible. So Raoul is on top, and Kenji (immediately west of Raoul, since the top row's right is west) is also on top. The top row is then Mads, Kenji, Raoul, Lola in some arrangement of seats 2 to 4, with Kenji immediately west of Raoul. Either Kenji 2 and Raoul 3 with Lola 4, or Lola 2 and Kenji 3 and Raoul 4. The second option puts Quillan opposite Lola in seat 2, which Pavla holds. So the first option: Top: Mads, Kenji, Raoul, Lola. Orsi (opposite Raoul) is bottom 3 and Quillan (opposite Lola) is bottom 4. Quillan faces north, so her left is west: bottom seat 3, **Orsi**.

**Q26** Ismail is seat 8 and Fiona, next to him, is seat 7. The remaining six people go in seats 1 to 6. Jun is 4 seats left of Dorit (exactly three between), so Dorit is seat 5 or 6. Gabor is right of Dorit, and seats 7 and 8 are taken, so Dorit cannot be 6; Dorit is seat 5, Jun is seat 1 and Gabor is seat 6. Seats 2, 3, 4 hold Hilda, Emeka and Kalani. Hilda, Emeka is a block, and Kalani is between Emeka and Dorit, so Kalani is seat 4, Hilda 2, Emeka 3. Order: Jun, Hilda, Emeka, Kalani, Dorit, Gabor, Fiona, Ismail. Between Hilda (2) and Gabor (6): 6 - 2 - 1 = **3** people.

**Q27** Going round: West, North 1, North 2, North 3, East, South 3, South 2, South 1. Magda is West, Rhonda East, Pedro North 1, Thea North 2. Nikhil is opposite Pedro, South 1. Stefan is on the north side and the only free north seat is North 3, so Stefan is North 3. Olive is opposite Stefan, South 3. Yannick takes South 2, which touches both South 3 (Olive) and South 1 (Nikhil), as clue 7 asks. Opposite Thea (North 2) is South 2, **Yannick**.

**Q28** Rebuild the starting queue. Rafi has three people ahead, so he is 4th; Odette is immediately ahead, 3rd. Luca is ahead of Odette, so in place 1 or 2, and Teo is ahead of Luca, so Teo is 1st and Luca 2nd. Saoirse is 7th. Maren and Nikos fill places 5 and 6 with Nikos behind Maren: Maren 5th, Nikos 6th. Start: Teo, Luca, Odette, Rafi, Maren, Nikos, Saoirse.
- Odette and Saoirse swap: Teo, Luca, Saoirse, Rafi, Maren, Nikos, Odette.
- First two leave: Saoirse, Rafi, Maren, Nikos, Odette.
- Uma then Vik join: Saoirse, Rafi, Maren, Nikos, Odette, Uma, Vik.
- Nikos swaps with the person behind him (Odette): Saoirse, Rafi, Maren, Odette, Nikos, Uma, Vik.
(a) 4th is **Odette**. (b) Rafi is 2nd and Nikos is 5th: 5 - 2 - 1 = **2** people (Maren and Odette).

**Q29** (a) Treat Ines, Jonas and Kai as one block. Now there are 4 things to arrange (the block and the three other people): 4 x 3 x 2 x 1 = 24 orders. Inside the block the three can be in any order: 3 x 2 x 1 = 6 ways. Total 24 x 6 = **144**. (Recounted by listing all 720 orders of six people: 144 have the three together.) (b) Fix Ines anywhere (turning the table changes nothing). Jonas must take the one seat directly opposite her: 1 way. The other four people fill the four remaining seats in 4 x 3 x 2 x 1 = 24 ways. Total **24**. (Recounted by script.)

**Q30** Number the seats anticlockwise (the right-hand direction) from Ewa, seat 0. Hugo is opposite: seat 3. Farouk is not a neighbour of Ewa, so Farouk is seat 2 or 4. Gwen and Hugo are both Tech, so Gwen is not a neighbour of Hugo: Gwen is not seat 2 or 4, so Gwen is seat 1 or 5. Gwen is immediately right of Inez, so Inez is the seat just before Gwen; if Gwen were seat 1, Inez would be seat 0, which Ewa holds. So Gwen is seat 5 and Inez seat 4. Farouk, not seat 4, is seat 2. Jamal takes seat 1. Order: Ewa, Jamal, Farouk, Hugo, Inez, Gwen. Check same-department neighbours: Ewa (0) and Farouk (2) are not neighbours; Hugo (3) and Gwen (5) are not; Inez (4) and Jamal (1) are not. Immediately right of Jamal (seat 1) is seat 2, **Farouk**.

## Chapter 17 answer key: Coding-Decoding and Alphabet/Number Logic

All answers were recomputed by script. Rule-finding questions (Q15, Q16, Q31 and examples 1 and 3) were checked against a family of about 1,500 candidate rules (constant, progressive and alternating shifts, with or without reversing the word); every rule that fits the examples gives the same answer. Sentence-code questions (Q18, Q19, Q32, Q38 and example 4) were checked by trying every possible word-to-code matching. Q35 was checked by trying every letter and digit combination.

### Self-check answers

1. CAT: C+1=D, A+1=B, T+1=U. **DBU**.
2. M is the 13th letter, so **13**.
3. B(2) to C(3), O(15) to P(16), X(24) to Y(25): every letter moves **1 place forward**.

### Easy

**Q1** Rule: +3. B(2)+3=5=E, A(1)+3=4=D, D(4)+3=7=G, G(7)+3=10=J, E(5)+3=8=H. **EDGJH**.

**Q2** Rule: -1. A(1)-1=0, which wraps to Z (26). B-1=A, O(15)-1=14=N, U(21)-1=20=T, T(20)-1=19=S. **ZANTS**.

**Q3** Mirror = 27 minus position. N(14) gives 13=M; A(1) gives 26=Z; M(13) gives 14=N; E(5) gives 22=V. **MZNV**.

**Q4** (a) H=8, I=9, R=18, E=5: **8 9 18 5**. (b) 20=T, 5=E, 1=A, 13=M: **TEAM**.

**Q5** Decode by moving back 2. J(10)-2=8=H, G(7)-2=5=E, N(14)-2=12=L, R(18)-2=16=P. **HELP**.

**Q6** First two letters of "chair": CH. Letters in "green": 5. Size: L. **CH5L**.

**Q7** Finance gives F, September is month 09, serial 14 written as 014. **F09014**.

**Q8** Swap the first and last letters. DESK: D and K swap, giving **KESD**. LEAVE: L and E swap, giving **EEAVL**.

**Q9** The thing you sit on is a chair. In this code language a chair is called a desk. **Desk**.

**Q10** Double the position. F(6) gives 12, A(1) gives 2, C(3) gives 6, E(5) gives 10. **12 2 6 10**.

**Q11** (a) LEDGER backwards: **REGDEL**. (b) TNEMYAP backwards: **PAYMENT**.

**Q12** PLANET splits into pairs PL, AN, ET. Swap inside each pair: LP, NA, TE. **LPNATE**.

**Q13** Differences: G(7) to J(10) is +3, A(1) to D(4) is +3, M(13) to P(16) is +3, E(5) to H(8) is +3. Rule +3. F(6)+3=9=I, I(9)+3=12=L, L(12)+3=15=O, E(5)+3=8=H. ILOH, option **(b)**. (Option (a) is +2 and (c) is +4, the typical off-by-one traps; (d) scrambles the last two letters.)

**Q14** Test each against the four conditions.
- KT482: two letters, three digits, first digit 4 (not 0), 4+8+2=14 (even). Valid.
- KT4820: four digits, fails.
- K4T82: a digit sits between the letters, fails the format.
- MP571: 5+7+1=13, odd, fails.
- RD064: first digit 0, fails.
**KT482** is the only valid one.

### Medium

**Q15** Differences for WORD to XQUH: X-W=1, Q-O=2, U-R=3, H-D=4. Progressive +1, +2, +3, +4. PLAN: P(16)+1=17=Q, L(12)+2=14=N, A(1)+3=4=D, N(14)+4=18=R. **QNDR**.

**Q16** STAFF to USCEH: S(19) to U(21) is +2; T(20) to S(19) is -1; A(1) to C(3) is +2; F(6) to E(5) is -1; F(6) to H(8) is +2. Alternating +2, -1, +2, -1, +2. CHART: C(3)+2=5=E, H(8)-1=7=G, A(1)+2=3=C, R(18)-1=17=Q, T(20)+2=22=V. **EGCQV**.

**Q17** Check the rule on TEAM: reverse gives MAET; mirror: M(13) to N(14), A to Z, E(5) to V(22), T(20) to G(7): NZVG. Matches. BONDS reversed is SDNOB. Mirrors: S(19) to 8=H, D(4) to 23=W, N(14) to 13=M, O(15) to 12=L, B(2) to 25=Y. **HWMLY**.

**Q18** "office closed today" and "closed holiday tomorrow" share "closed"; the code words they share are "ma", so closed = ma. "closed holiday tomorrow" and "tomorrow office open" share "tomorrow"; shared code "du", so tomorrow = du. The first and third sentences share "office"; shared code "fi", so office = fi. In sentence 2 the codes are du, ma, ve; closed = ma and tomorrow = du are used, so holiday = **ve**.

**Q19** Shared words and shared codes:
- Sentences 1 and 2 share "staff"; shared code ti, so staff = ti.
- Sentences 1 and 3 share "joined"; shared code ba, so joined = ba.
- Sentences 1 and 4 share "new"; shared code mo, so new = mo.
- Sentences 2 and 3 share "team"; shared code ru, so team = ru.
- Sentences 2 and 4 share "today"; shared code ne, so today = ne.
- Sentence 3: joined = ba, team = ru, so "hired" is the leftover code **pa**.
- Sentence 4: new = mo, today = ne, so "left" is the leftover code **ko**.

**Q20** HIRE: 8+9+18+5=40. FIRE: 6+9+18+5=38. WAGE: 23+1+7+5=36. LATE: 12+1+20+5=38. Largest: **HIRE (40)**. Equal: **FIRE and LATE (38 each)**. (Different words can have the same value, a first look at collisions.)

**Q21** Surname first two letters, birth month (2 digits), last letter of first name.
- Rohan Menon: ME, 03, N: **ME03N**.
- Anita Pandey: PA, 11, A: **PA11A**.
- Karan Mehta: ME, 03, N: **ME03N**.
- Priya Rao: RA, 07, A: **RA07A**.
Rohan and Karan clash (**ME03N** twice), so the ID cannot tell them apart. One fix: add a serial number at the end (ME03N01, ME03N02).

**Q22** (a) WL = wallet, S = small, GN = green, 120 grams: **a small green wallet of 120 grams**. (b) Bag = BK, large = L, blue = BL, weight 400: **BK-L-BL-400**.

**Q23** Try squares: 2 squared is 4, plus 1 is 5; 3 squared is 9, plus 1 is 10; 5 squared is 25, plus 1 is 26; 6 squared is 36, plus 1 is 37. The rule "square then add 1" fits all four. For 9: 81+1=**82**. (A computer check of all rules of the form a x n squared + b x n + c shows this rule is the only one that fits the four examples.)

**Q24** C stays a consonant: C to D. U is a vowel: *. S to T. T to U. O is a vowel: *. M to N. **D*TU*N**.

**Q25** HIRE to KLUH: H(8) to K(11), I(9) to L(12), R(18) to U(21), E(5) to H(8): +3 each. To decode, go back 3. Z(26)-3=23=W, R(18)-3=15=O, U(21)-3=18=R, N(14)-3=11=K. **WORK**.

**Q26** Sum of first five digits, then units digit:
- 483925: 4+8+3+9+2=26, units 6, sixth digit 5. Not valid.
- 120744: 1+2+0+7+4=14, units 4, sixth digit 4. Valid.
- 907651: 9+0+7+6+5=27, units 7, sixth digit 1. Not valid.
- 555550: 5+5+5+5+5=25, units 5, sixth digit 0. Not valid.
- 341682: 3+4+1+6+8=22, units 2, sixth digit 2. Valid.
**2 are valid** (120744 and 341682).

**Q27** Keys: HIRE is 4 (H), 4 (I), 7 (R), 3 (E) = 4473. GIRD is 4 (G), 4 (I), 7 (R), 3 (D) = 4473. BONUS is 26687, STAFF is 78233, PAY is 729, all different. **HIRE and GIRD, code 4473.** (They share a key for every letter, so the keypad code cannot be decoded to one word.)

**Q28** Build the lookup: the cipher alphabet written under A to Z is B U D G E T A C F H I J K L M N O P Q R S V W X Y Z.
(a) N in the cipher line is under the plain letter P (16th), B under A, Y under Y; so NBY = **PAY**. M is under O, L under N, so ML = **ON**. T is under F, P under R, F under I, G under D, B under A, Y under Y; so TPFGBY = **FRIDAY**. Message: **PAY ON FRIDAY**.
(b) S is 19th plain; the 19th cipher letter is Q. E (5th) goes to E (5th cipher letter). N (14th) goes to L. D (4th) goes to G. SEND = **QELG**.

**Q29** (Letters, vowels): PAYMENT (7, 2: A, E); LEAVE (5, 3: E, A, E); RECRUIT (7, 3: E, U, I); TENURE (6, 3: E, U, E); BENEFIT (7, 3: E, E, I); SHIFT (5, 1: I). **RECRUIT and BENEFIT share (7, 3).**

**Q30** (a) B(2) is even, half is 1. A(1) is odd, plus 1 is 2. D(4) is even, half is 2. G(7) is odd, plus 1 is 8. E(5) is odd, plus 1 is 6. **1 2 2 8 6**.
(b) D gives 2. A gives 2 as well (1+1). Any other? Odd n gives n+1 = 2 only for n=1; even n gives n/2 = 2 only for n=4. So **A and D**.
(c) Two different letters can give the same number, so the code cannot be decoded with certainty (for example, 2 could be A or D). An ID code must come from only one source, so this is a poor choice.

### Hard

**Q31** Test the idea "reverse the word, then shift progressively +1, +2, +3, +4". HIRE reversed is ERIH: E(5)+1=6=F, R(18)+2=20=T, I(9)+3=12=L, H(8)+4=12=L: FTLL. Matches. LEAD reversed is DAEL: D(4)+1=5=E, A(1)+2=3=C, E(5)+3=8=H, L(12)+4=16=P: ECHP. Matches. TRAIN reversed is NIART: N(14)+1=15=O, I(9)+2=11=K, A(1)+3=4=D, R(18)+4=22=V, T(20)+5=25=Y. **OKDVY**.

**Q32** Shared words, using the codes:
- Sentences 1 and 2 share two words (budget, review) and two codes (ha, zi), but we do not yet know which is which.
- Sentences 3 and 4 share one code, **ko**, so "urgent" = ko.
- Sentence 4 and sentence 1 share "review", with shared code **ha**. So review = ha.
- Then "budget" is the other shared code of sentences 1 and 2, which is **zi**.
- Sentence 4 has review (ha), urgent (ko), so "final" is the leftover **ro**.
Answer: **final = ro, budget = zi**. (Trying all matchings finds exactly one fit.)

**Q33** (a) LOYAL: L(12), O(15), Y(25), A(1), L(12). Places 1, 3, 5: 12+25+12=49. Places 2, 4: 15+1=16. Value 49-16=**33**.
(b) GRADE: G(7)+A(1)+E(5)=13; R(18)+D(4)=22; 13-22=-9. CADRE: C(3)+D(4)+E(5)=12; A(1)+R(18)=19; 12-19=-7. STAFF: S(19)+A(1)+F(6)=26; T(20)+F(6)=26; 26-26=0. LEAD: L(12)+A(1)=13; E(5)+D(4)=9; 13-9=4. **STAFF** has value 0.

**Q34** The shift: S(19) to Z(26) is +7. Checking the rest of SEND: E(5) to L(12) is +7, N(14) to U(21) is +7, D(4) to K(11) is +7. So the rule is a constant +7 (checked against all 26 possible shifts: only +7 turns SEND into ZLUK). To decode, move back 7 (wrapping before A to Z).
- AOL: A(1)-7 wraps to 20=T, O(15)-7=8=H, L(12)-7=5=E. THE.
- VMMLY: V(22)-7=15=O, M(13)-7=6=F, M gives F, L(12)-7=5=E, Y(25)-7=18=R. OFFER.
- SLAALY: S(19)-7=12=L, L(12)-7=5=E, A gives T, A gives T, L gives E, Y gives R. LETTER.
- AVKHF: A gives T, V(22)-7=15=O, K(11)-7=4=D, H(8)-7=1=A, F(6)-7 wraps to 25=Y. TODAY.
Message: **SEND THE OFFER LETTER TODAY**.

**Q35** Clue 2: vowels are A, E, I, O, U with positions 1, 5, 9, 15, 21; multiples of 5 are E (5) and O (15) only. Clue 1: the mirror of E is V (22), which is later in the alphabet, fine; the mirror of O is L (12), which is earlier, so O fails clue 2. So the letters are **EV**. Digits: equal gaps with a sum of 18 means the middle digit is 18/3=6. First digit a, last digit 12-a. Clue 5: last = 3 x first, so 12-a=3a, a=3, last 9. Digits: 3, 6, 9 (all different, increasing, gaps of 3). Code: **EV369**. (Checked by trying every letter and every digit combination: this is the only code fitting all five clues.)

**Q36** Subtract the place from each number. 19-1=18=R, 17-2=15=O, 22-3=19=S, 24-4=20=T, 10-5=5=E, 24-6=18=R. **ROSTER**.

**Q37** Rules: R1 region in N, S, E, W. R2 category in T, B, F. R3 size 28 to 46. R4 check digit = units digit of 3 x (sum of size digits).
- NT367: N ok, T ok, size 36 ok, check: (3+6) x 3=27, units 7, given 7. **Valid.**
- QT325: Q is not a region letter. Breaks **R1** (the size 32 and check digit 5 are fine: (3+2) x 3=15).
- EK367: K is not a category. Breaks **R2** (size 36 and check 7 are fine).
- WB505: size 50 is outside 28 to 46. Breaks **R3** (the check digit is fine: (5+0) x 3=15, units 5).
- NF395: size 39 is fine, check should be (3+9) x 3=36, units 6, but the code shows 5. Breaks **R4**.
Only **NT367** is valid.

**Q38** Sentences 1 and 2 share two words (grade, band) and two codes (ju, ki). Nothing in the three sentences separates them: grade and band appear together in exactly the same sentences. So "grade" is either ju or ki and **cannot be determined**. Check by trying all matchings: two different matchings fit (grade = ju, band = ki, or the other way round). For "cap": sentence 3 has ta, wo, re. The code ta is shared by sentences 1 and 3, so pay = ta; the code wo is shared by sentences 2 and 3, so bonus = wo. That leaves re for cap, so "cap" = **re**, which is certain in both matchings.

**Q39** (a) CLAIM has 5 letters, so +5. C(3)+5=8=H, L(12)+5=17=Q, A(1)+5=6=F, I(9)+5=14=N, M(13)+5=18=R. **HQFNR**.
(b) The coded word has 4 letters, so the plain word was shifted by 4. Go back 4. X(24)-4=20=T, I(9)-4=5=E, E(5)-4=1=A, Q(17)-4=13=M. **TEAM**.

**Q40** (a) First letters A and V. Mirrors: A(1) to Z(26); V(22) to 27-22=5=E. So ZE. Year 2021 gives 21. Finance is 3. Check: 2+1+3=6, units 6. ID: **ZE2136**.
(b) Rahul Singh: R(18) mirrors to 9=I; S(19) mirrors to 8=H. IH. Year 2019 gives 19. Sales is 2. Check: 1+9+2=12, units 2. Correct ID: **IH1922**. The sheet shows IH1923, whose check digit is 3, so it is **not correct**; it should be IH1922.
